Sawant Address: Department of Mathematics, Indian Institute of Technology (IIT) Madras, India Email address: sagar@smail.iitm.ac.in and Samir Shukla Address: School of Mathematical and Statistical Sciences, Indian Institute of Technology (IIT) Mandi, India Email address: samir@iitmandi.ac.in Abstract. The matching complex 𝖬 ⁡ ( G ) \mathsf{M}(G) of a graph G G is a simplicial complex whose simplices are matchings in G G . These complexes appear in various places and found applications in many areas of mathematics including computational geometry, representation theory, combinatorics, etc. In this article, we consider the matching complexes of the categorical product P n × P m P_{n}\times P_{m} of path graphs P n P_{n} and P m P_{m} . For m = 1 m=1 , P n × P m P_{n}\times P_{m} is a discrete graph and therefore its matching complex is the void complex. For m = 2 m=2 , 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n × P m ) \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\times P_{m}) has been proved to be homotopy equivalent to a wedge of spheres by Kozlov. We show that for n ≥ 2 n\geq 2 and 3 ≤ m ≤ 5 3\leq m\leq 5 , the matching complex of P n × P m P_{n}\times P_{m} is homotopy equivalent to a wedge of spheres. For m = 3 m=3 , we explicitly compute the number and dimension of spheres appearing in the wedge. Furthermore, for m ∈ { 4 , 5 } m\in\{4,5\} , we provide the minimum and maximum dimensions of spheres appearing in the wedge in the homotopy type of 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n × P m ) \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\times P_{m}) . Key words and phrases: matching complex, independence complex, categorical product of graphs, path graph, line graph 2020 Mathematics Subject Classification 55P10, 05E45, 55U10 1 Introduction A matching in a simple graph G G is a collection of pairwise disjoint edges. The matching complex 𝖬 ⁡ ( G ) \mathsf{M}(G) of a graph G G is the simplicial complex whose vertices are the edges, and simplicies are the matchings in G G . In topological combinatorics, it is an important problem to study the topology of matching complexes of graphs. Matching complexes first appeared in the thesis of Garst [ Gar79 ] , where they obtained the matching complexes of complete bipartite graphs K m , n K_{m,n} as special coset complexes of symmetric groups S n S_{n} . Thereafter, matching complexes have appeared and found applications in various places. The matching complexes of complete graphs were studied in the paper of Bouc [ Bou92 ] in connection with Brown complexes and Quillen complexes. In [ BLVv94 ] , Björner et al., described the matching complexes of complete bipartite graphs as chessboard complexes to study the geometry of non-attacking rook configurations on a general m × n m\times n chessboard. In [ vV92 ] , 𝖬 ⁡ ( K m , n ) \mathsf{M}(K_{m,n}) appeared as the complex of all partial injective functions from { 1 , … , m } \{1,\ldots,m\} to { 1 , … , n } \{1,\ldots,n\} in connection to the problem of estimating the number of halving hyperplanes of a finite set of points in Euclidean space. Other than the complete graphs and complete bipartite graphs, the classes of graphs for which matching complexes have been studied include; paths and cycles [ Koz99 ] , trees [ MT08 , MJMV22 ] , polygonal tilings [ Mat22 , BJMV23 ] , honeycomb graphs [ MT08 ] , etc. For more details on these complexes, we refer to a survey article by Wachs [ Wac03 ] and Chapter 11 of the book [ Jon08 ] by Jonsson. Definition 1.1 . The cartesian product G ​ □ ​ H G\Box H of graphs G G and H H is the graph with vertex set V ⁡ ( G ) × V ⁡ ( H ) V(G)\times V(H) , where any two vertices ( u , v ) (u,v) and ( u ′ , v ′ ) (u^{\prime},v^{\prime}) form an edge if and only if either u = u ′ u=u^{\prime} and v ​ v ′ ∈ E ⁡ ( H ) vv^{\prime}\in E(H) , or v = v ′ v=v^{\prime} and u ​ u ′ ∈ E ⁡ ( G ) uu^{\prime}\in E(G) . Definition 1.2 . The categorical product G × H G\times H of graphs G G and H H is the graph with vertex set V ⁡ ( G ) × V ⁡ ( H ) V(G)\times V(H) , where any two vertices ( u , v ) (u,v) and ( u ′ , v ′ ) (u^{\prime},v^{\prime}) form an edge if and only if u ​ u ′ ∈ E ⁡ ( G ) uu^{\prime}\in E(G) and v ​ v ′ ∈ E ⁡ ( H ) vv^{\prime}\in E(H) . For n ≥ 1 n\geq 1 , let P n P_{n} denote the path graph on n n vertices. The matching complexes of the cartesian product of path graphs have been studied by various authors. In [ Koz99 ] , Kozlov studied 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n ​ □ ​ P 1 ) \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\Box P_{1}) . In [ Jon05 ] , Jonsson studied the homotopical depth and topological connectivity of matching complexes of the cartesian product of path graphs for certain cases. Furthermore, Braun and Hough investigated the homology of 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n ​ □ ​ P 2 ) \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\Box P_{2}) in [ BH17 ] , and its homotopy type was examined by Matsushita in [ Mat19 ] . Motivated by the cartesian product of path graphs, in this article, we consider the matching complexes of the categorical product P n × P m P_{n}\times P_{m} of path graphs (see Figure 1 ). Since P n × P 1 P_{n}\times P_{1} does not contain any edge, 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n × P 1 ) \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\times P_{1}) is a void complex. Further, P n × P 2 P_{n}\times P_{2} is a disjoint union of two path graphs, and therefore 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n × P 2 ) \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\times P_{2}) is homotopy equivalent to a wedge of spheres from [ Koz99 ] . For n ≥ 2 n\geq 2 and 3 ≤ m ≤ 5 3\leq m\leq 5 , 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n × P m ) \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\times P_{m}) is homotopy equivalent to a wedge of spheres. We explicitly compute the dimension and number of spheres in the homotopy type of 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n × P 3 ) \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\times P_{3}) (see Theorem 3.5 ). For m ∈ { 4 , 5 } m\in\{4,5\} , we give the minimum and maximum dimension of spheres appearing in the wedge in homotopy type of 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n × P m ) \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\times P_{m}) . Proposition 1.4 . The line graph L ⁡ ( G ) L(G) of a graph G G is the graph whose vertices are the edges of G G , and any two such distinct vertices u ​ v uv and u ′ ​ v ′ u^{\prime}v^{\prime} are joined by an edge in L ⁡ ( G ) L(G) if and only if they have a common endpoint, i.e. , if { u , v } ∩ { u ′ , v ′ } ≠ ∅ \{u,v\}\cap\{u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\}\neq\emptyset . For a graph G G , a subset A ⊆ V ⁡ ( G ) A\subseteq V(G) is said to be independent in G G if the induced subgraph on A A does not contain any edge. The independence complex Ind ⁡ ( G ) \mathrm{Ind}(G) of G G is a simplicial complex on V ⁡ ( G ) V(G) whose simplices are the independent sets in G G . Observe that for any graph G G , the matching complex 𝖬 ⁡ ( G ) \mathsf{M}(G) is the same as the Ind ⁡ ( L ⁡ ( G ) ) \mathrm{Ind}(L(G)) . To prove Theorem 1.3 , we show that Ind ⁡ ( L ⁡ ( P n × P m ) ) \mathrm{Ind}(L(P_{n}\times P_{m})) is a wedge of spheres up to homotopy equivalence. The main idea used in this proof is to reduce the graph L ⁡ ( P n × P m ) L(P_{n}\times P_{m}) into smaller graphs by the operations; deletions of vertices, deletions of edges, and additions of edges. During this process of reduction, we come across thirteen new classes of graphs in the case m = 5 m=5 , four new classes of graphs in the case m = 4 m=4 , and one new class of graphs in the case m = 3 m=3 . We establish recursive relations among the independence complex of L ⁡ ( P n × P m ) L(P_{n}\times P_{m}) and independence complexes of these new classes of graphs. This paper is organized as follows: In Section 2 , we present the necessary preliminaries on graph theory and simplicial complexes. In Section 3 , we determine the homotopy type of the matching complexes of P n × P 3 P_{n}\times P_{3} , showing that it is a wedge of spheres, and we explicitly compute both the number and the dimensions of the spheres appearing in the wedge. In Section 4 , we compute the homotopy type of the matching complexes of P n × P 4 P_{n}\times P_{4} . The graph P n × P 4 P_{n}\times P_{4} is disconnected and consists of two connected components. Regardless of whether n n is odd or even, these components are isomorphic. For n ≥ 0 n\geq 0 , we denote the connected components of the line graphs of P 2 ​ n + 2 × P 4 P_{2n+2}\times P_{4} and P 2 ​ n + 3 × P 4 P_{2n+3}\times P_{4} by Γ n , 4 \Gamma_{n,4} (see Figure 4(a) ) and Γ ~ n , 4 \widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,4} (see Figure 4(b) ), respectively. In Section 4.1 , we formally define the graphs Γ n , 4 \Gamma_{n,4} , Γ ~ n , 4 \widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,4} , and four intermediary graphs needed to compute the homotopy types of the independence complexes of Γ n , 4 \Gamma_{n,4} and Γ ~ n , 4 \widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,4} . In Section 4.2 , we establish recursive relations between their independence complexes. Then, in Section 4.3 , we conclude the homotopy type of the matching complexes of P n × P 4 P_{n}\times P_{4} , showing that it is a wedge of spheres, and we determine the maximum and minimum dimensions of spheres that occur in the wedge in 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n × P 4 ) \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\times P_{4}) . Section 5 is majorly dedicated to the matching complexes of P n × P 5 P_{n}\times P_{5} and is divided into three subsections. The graph L ⁡ ( P n × P 5 ) L(P_{n}\times P_{5}) is disconnected and has exactly two components. For k ≥ 0 k\geq 0 , if n = 2 ​ k + 2 n=2k+2 , then L ⁡ ( P n × P 5 ) L(P_{n}\times P_{5}) consists of two copies of the graph Γ k , 5 \Gamma_{k,5} (see Figure 10 ); and if n = 2 ​ k + 3 n=2k+3 , then L ⁡ ( P n × P 5 ) = Λ k , 5 ⊔ Γ ~ k , 5 L(P_{n}\times P_{5})=\Lambda_{k,5}\sqcup\widetilde{\Gamma}_{k,5} (see Figure 11 and Figure 12 ). We start Section 5 with the definitions of the graphs Γ k , 5 , Λ k , 5 \Gamma_{k,5},\Lambda_{k,5} and Γ ~ k , 5 \widetilde{\Gamma}_{k,5} . In Section 5.1 , we compute the independence complexes of Γ k , 5 \Gamma_{k,5} and Λ k , 5 \Lambda_{k,5} . We introduce 6 6 new classes of graphs (which we need in the computation of Ind ⁡ ( Γ k , 5 ) \mathrm{Ind}(\Gamma_{k,5}) and Ind ⁡ ( Λ k , 5 ) \mathrm{Ind}(\Lambda_{k,5}) ), and establish recursive relations among independence complexes of graphs Γ k , 5 , Λ k , 5 \Gamma_{k,5},\Lambda_{k,5} and these 6 6 classes of graphs. In Section 5.2 , we compute the independence complex of the graph Γ ~ k , 5 \widetilde{\Gamma}_{k,5} . Here again, we define another 7 7 class of graphs (which we need in the computation of Ind ⁡ ( Γ ~ k , 5 ) \mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{k,5}) ), and establish recursive relations among independence complexes of these 7 7 new classes of graphs and Ind ⁡ ( Γ ~ k , 5 ) \mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{k,5}) . In Section 5.3 , we conclude the homotopy type of 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n × P 5 ) \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\times P_{5}) and determine the minimum and maximum dimensions of spheres appearing in the wedge in 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n × P 5 ) \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\times P_{5}) . Finally, in Section 6 , we pose a few questions and a conjecture, which naturally arise from the work done in this paper. 2 Preliminaries A graph G G is an ordered pair ( V ⁡ ( G ) , E ⁡ ( G ) ) (V(G),E(G)) , where V ⁡ ( G ) V(G) is a finite set of vertices and E ⁡ ( G ) ⊆ ( V ⁡ ( G ) 2 ) E(G)\subseteq\binom{V(G)}{2} is the set of edges of G G , consisting of 2 2 -element subsets of V ⁡ ( G ) V(G) . For the sake of brevity, we denote an edge { u , v } \{u,v\} by u ​ v uv . Given a graph G = ( V ⁡ ( G ) , E ⁡ ( G ) ) G=(V(G),E(G)) and a vertex u u in V ⁡ ( G ) V(G) , the open neighborhood of u u is defined as the collection { v ∈ V ⁡ ( G ) : u ​ v ∈ E ⁡ ( G ) } \{v\in V(G):uv\in E(G)\} and it is denoted by N ⁡ ( u ) N(u) . The closed neighborhood of a vertex u u in the graph G G is defined as { u } ∪ N ⁡ ( u ) \{u\}\cup N(u) , and is denoted by N ⁡ [ u ] N[u] . A map f : V ⁡ ( G ) → V ⁡ ( H ) f\colon V(G)\rightarrow V(H) is said to be a graph homomorphism if f ⁡ ( v ) ​ f ​ ( w ) ∈ E ⁡ ( H ) f(v)f(w)\in E(H) for all v ​ w ∈ E ⁡ ( G ) vw\in E(G) . A bijective graph homomorphism is called an isomorphism . If such an isomorphism exists between two graphs G G and H H , they are called isomorphic , denoted as G ≅ H G\cong H . Let S = { v 1 , … , v r } S=\{v_{1},\dots,v_{r}\} be a collection of vertices in V ⁡ ( G ) V(G) . Then, G ∖ S G\setminus S denotes the induced subgraph of G G on the vertex set V ⁡ ( G ) ∖ S V(G)\setminus S . In particular, if S S is the singleton set { v 1 } \{v_{1}\} , then we write the graph G ∖ S G\setminus S as G ∖ v 1 G\setminus v_{1} . Similarly, for a collection of edges T = { e 1 , … , e s } T=\{e_{1},\dots,e_{s}\} , by G + T G+T and G − T G-T we denote the graphs on the vertex set V ⁡ ( G ) V(G) with edge set E ⁡ ( G ) ∪ T E(G)\cup T and E ⁡ ( G ) ∖ T E(G)\setminus T , respectively. For more details about the graphs, we refer the reader to [ Wes01 ] . An (abstract) simplicial complex Δ \Delta on a vertex set V V is a collection of subsets of V V such that if σ ∈ Δ \sigma\in\Delta and τ \tau is a subset of σ \sigma , then τ ∈ Δ \tau\in\Delta . If σ ∈ Δ \sigma\in\Delta and | σ | = k + 1 |\sigma|=k+1 , then we say σ \sigma is a simplex of dimension k k or a k k -simplex. We further assume that the empty set is present in every simplicial complex as the only simplex of dimension − 1 -1 . The 0 0 -dimensional simplices are called the vertices of Δ \Delta , and the set of all vertices of a simplicial complex Δ \Delta is denoted by V ⁡ ( Δ ) V(\Delta) . A subcomplex of a simplicial complex is a sub-collection of simplices that itself forms a simplicial complex. If Δ \Delta is a simplicial complex and σ \sigma is a simplex in Δ \Delta , then the link and deletion of σ \sigma are defined as the subcomplexes { τ ∈ Δ : σ ∪ τ ∈ Δ ​ and ​ σ ∩ τ = ∅ } \{\tau\in\Delta:\sigma\cup\tau\in\Delta\hskip 5.69046pt\text{and}\hskip 2.84544pt\sigma\cap\tau=\emptyset\} and { τ ∈ Δ : σ ⊈ τ } \{\tau\in\Delta:\sigma\nsubseteq\tau\} , respectively. The join of two simplicial complexes Δ 1 \Delta_{1} and Δ 2 \Delta_{2} , where V ⁡ ( Δ 1 ) ∩ V ⁡ ( Δ 2 ) = ∅ V(\Delta_{1})\cap V(\Delta_{2})=\emptyset , is the simplicial complex Δ 1 ∗ Δ 2 ≔ { σ ∪ τ : σ ∈ Δ 1 ​ and ​ τ ∈ Δ 2 } \Delta_{1}\ast\Delta_{2}\coloneq\{\sigma\cup\tau:\sigma\in\Delta_{1}\hskip 5.69046pt\text{and}\hskip 2.84544pt\tau\in\Delta_{2}\} . In particular, if a , b a,b are vertices that are not part of a simplicial complex Δ \Delta , then the simplicial complexes { a } ∗ Δ \{a\}\ast\Delta and ( { a } ∗ Δ ) ∪ ( { b } ∗ Δ ) (\{a\}\ast\Delta)\cup(\{b\}\ast\Delta) are called the cone and the suspension of Δ \Delta with the apexes a a and { a , b } \{a,b\} , respectively. We denote the suspension of Δ \Delta with apex { a , b } \{a,b\} by Σ a , b ​ Δ \Sigma_{a,b}\Delta (or Σ ​ Δ \Sigma\Delta ). In this article, we consider any simplicial complex as a topological space, namely its geometric realization. For the definition of geometric realization, we refer the reader to [ Koz08 ] . For details about topological background, one may refer to [ Hat02 ] . Proposition 2.1 . Let G 1 ⊔ G 2 G_{1}\sqcup G_{2} denote the disjoint union of two graphs, G 1 G_{1} and G 2 G_{2} . Then Ind ⁡ ( G 1 ⊔ G 2 ) ≃ Ind ⁡ ( G 1 ) ∗ Ind ⁡ ( G 2 ) . \mathrm{Ind}(G_{1}\sqcup G_{2})\simeq\mathrm{Ind}(G_{1})\ast\mathrm{Ind}(G_{2}). Clearly, for each vertex v ∈ V ⁡ ( G ) v\in V(G) , the link and the deletion of the simplex { v } \{v\} in Ind ⁡ ( G ) \mathrm{Ind}(G) are the same as Ind ⁡ ( G ∖ N ⁡ [ v ] ) \mathrm{Ind}(G\setminus N[v]) and Ind ⁡ ( G ∖ v ) \mathrm{Ind}(G\setminus v) , respectively. Observe that Ind ⁡ ( G ) \mathrm{Ind}(G) is the same as the simplicial complex ( { v } ∗ Ind ⁡ ( G ∖ N ⁡ [ v ] ) ) ∪ Ind ⁡ ( G ∖ v ) (\{v\}\ast\mathrm{Ind}(G\setminus N[v]))\cup\mathrm{Ind}(G\setminus v) . Further, from [ Ada12 ] , we have the following: Proposition 2.2 (Proposition 3.1, [ Ada12 ] ) . Let v v be a vertex of a graph G G . If the inclusion Ind ⁡ ( G ∖ N ⁡ [ v ] ) ↪ Ind ⁡ ( G ∖ v ) \mathrm{Ind}(G\setminus N[v])\xhookrightarrow{}\mathrm{Ind}(G\setminus v) is null-homotopic, then we have Ind ⁡ ( G ) ≃ Ind ⁡ ( G ∖ v ) ∨ Σ ​ Ind ​ ( G ∖ N ⁡ [ v ] ) . \mathrm{Ind}(G)\simeq\mathrm{Ind}(G\setminus v)\vee\Sigma\mathrm{Ind}(G\setminus N[v]). Proposition 2.3 (Lemma 2.4, [ Eng08 ] ) . Let v v and w w be a pair of distinct vertices of G G with N ⁡ ( v ) ⊆ N ⁡ ( w ) N(v)\subseteq N(w) . Then Ind ⁡ ( G ) ≃ Ind ⁡ ( G ∖ w ) \mathrm{Ind}(G)\simeq{}\mathrm{Ind}(G\setminus w) . Proposition 2.4 (Lemma 2.5, [ Eng08 ] ) . Let G G be a graph and v v be a simplicial vertex of G G , that is, all neighbors of v v are pairwise adjacent. Let N ⁡ ( v ) = { w 1 , w 2 , … , w k } N(v)=\{w_{1},w_{2},\dots,w_{k}\} . Then Ind ⁡ ( G ) ≃ ⋁ i = 1 k Σ ​ Ind ​ ( G ∖ N ⁡ [ w i ] ) . \mathrm{Ind}(G)\simeq\bigvee_{i=1}^{k}\Sigma\mathrm{Ind}(G\setminus N[w_{i}]). Proposition 2.5 (Proposition 3.4, [ Ada12 ] ) . Let G G be a graph and let u ​ v uv be an edge in G G . If Ind ⁡ ( G ∖ N ⁡ [ u , v ] ) \mathrm{Ind}(G\setminus N[u,v]) is contractible, then Ind ⁡ ( G ) ≃ Ind ⁡ ( G ∖ u ​ v ) . \mathrm{Ind}(G)\simeq\mathrm{Ind}(G\setminus uv). Definition 2.6 . A triplet of distinct vertices u , v u,v and x x in a graph G G satisfying x ∉ N ⁡ ( u ) ∪ N ⁡ ( v ) x\notin N(u)\cup N(v) and N ⁡ ( x ) ⊆ N ⁡ ( u ) ∪ N ⁡ ( v ) N(x)\subseteq N(u)\cup N(v) is called an edge-invariant triplet in G G , and denoted by [ u , v ; x ] [u,v;x] . Based on the Proposition 2.5 , we have the following. Suppose G G is a graph that consists of vertices { u , v } \{u,v\} and some vertex x x (depending on u u and v v ) such that [ u , v ; x ] [u,v;x] is an edge invariant triplet in G G . Then x x is an isolated vertex in G ∖ N ⁡ [ u , v ] G\setminus N[u,v] , which implies that Ind ⁡ ( G ∖ N ⁡ [ u , v ] ) \mathrm{Ind}(G\setminus N[u,v]) is contractible. Thus, we have Ind ⁡ ( G ) ≃ Ind ⁡ ( G ​ △ ​ u ​ v ) , \mathrm{Ind}(G)\simeq\mathrm{Ind}(G\triangle uv), where V ⁡ ( G ​ △ ​ u ​ v ) = V ⁡ ( G ) V(G\triangle uv)=V(G) , and E ⁡ ( G ​ △ ​ u ​ v ) E(G\triangle uv) is given by the symmetric difference E ⁡ ( G ) ​ △ ​ { u ​ v } . E(G)\triangle\{uv\}. For n ≥ 3 n\geq 3 , Ind ⁡ ( 𝒞 n ) ≃ { 𝕊 k − 1 ∨ S k − 1 if ​ n = 3 ​ k , 𝕊 k − 1 if ​ n = 3 ​ k ± 1 . \mathrm{Ind}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\simeq\begin{cases}\mathbb{S}^{k-1}\vee S^{k-1}&\text{if}\ n=3k,\\ \mathbb{S}^{k-1}&\text{if}\ n=3k\pm 1.\\ \end{cases} 3 Matching complexes of P n × P 3 P_{n}\times P_{3} In this section, we determine the homotopy type of the matching complex of P n × P 3 P_{n}\times P_{3} . The graph P n × P 3 P_{n}\times P_{3} is disconnected and has two connected components. When n n is even, then these components are isomorphic. However, for n n odd, the components are not isomorphic. For n ≥ 0 n\geq 0 , we denote the (isomorphic) connected components of the line graphs of P 2 ​ n + 2 × P 3 P_{2n+2}\times P_{3} by Γ n , 3 \Gamma_{n,3} (see Figure 2(a) ). Furthermore, for n ≥ 0 n\geq 0 , we denote the (non-isomorphic) connected components of the line graphs of P 2 ​ n + 3 × P 3 P_{2n+3}\times P_{3} by Λ n , 3 \Lambda_{n,3} and Γ ~ n , 3 \widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,3} (see Figures 3(a) and 3(b) ), respectively. Therefore, we have the following: 𝖬 ⁡ ( P 2 ​ n + 2 × P 3 ) ≃ Ind ⁡ ( Γ n , 3 ) ∗ Ind ⁡ ( Γ n , 3 ) ​ and ​ 𝖬 ​ ( P 2 ​ n + 3 × P 3 ) ≃ Ind ⁡ ( Λ n , 3 ) ∗ Ind ⁡ ( Γ ~ n , 3 ) . \mathsf{M}(P_{2n+2}\times P_{3})\simeq\mathrm{Ind}(\Gamma_{n,3})*\mathrm{Ind}(\Gamma_{n,3})\ \text{and}\ \mathsf{M}(P_{2n+3}\times P_{3})\simeq\mathrm{Ind}(\Lambda_{n,3})*\mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,3}). During the computation of the homotopy type of the independence complexes of graphs Γ n , 3 , Λ n , 3 \Gamma_{n,3},\Lambda_{n,3} , and Γ ~ n , 3 \widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,3} for n ≥ 0 n\geq 0 , we encounter an intermediary graph, denoted as A n , 3 A_{n,3} (see Figure 2(b) ). Using the independence complex of A n , 3 A_{n,3} , we illustrate that the independence complexes of Γ n , 3 , Λ n , 3 \Gamma_{n,3},\Lambda_{n,3} , and Γ ~ n , 3 \widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,3} are the wedges of spheres. Claim 3.1 . For n ≥ 0 n\geq 0 , Ind ⁡ ( Γ n , 3 ) ≃ 𝕊 n \mathrm{Ind}(\Gamma_{n,3})\simeq\mathbb{S}^{n} . Proof. Claim 3.3 . For n ≥ 0 n\geq 0 , Ind ⁡ ( Λ n , 3 ) ≃ ⋁ 2 n + 2 − 1 𝕊 n . \mathrm{Ind}(\Lambda_{n,3})\simeq\bigvee^{2^{n+2}-1}\mathbb{S}^{n}. Proof. The proof is by induction on n n . For n ≥ 0 n\geq 0 , 𝖬 ⁡ ( P 2 ​ n + 2 × P 3 ) ≃ 𝕊 2 ​ n + 1 \mathsf{M}(P_{2n+2}\times P_{3})\simeq\mathbb{S}^{2n+1} and 𝖬 ⁡ ( P 2 ​ n + 3 × P 3 ) ≃ ⋁ 2 n + 2 − 1 𝕊 2 ​ n + 1 . \mathsf{M}(P_{2n+3}\times P_{3})\simeq\bigvee^{2^{n+2}-1}\mathbb{S}^{2n+1}. Proof. Since the line graph of the categorical product P 2 ​ n + 2 × P 3 P_{2n+2}\times P_{3} is the disjoint union of two copies of Γ n , 3 \Gamma_{n,3} , using Proposition 2.1 and Claim 3.1 , we get 𝖬 ⁡ ( P 2 ​ n + 2 × P 3 ) ≃ Ind ⁡ ( Γ n , 3 ) ∗ Ind ⁡ ( Γ n , 3 ) ≃ 𝕊 2 ​ n + 1 . \mathsf{M}(P_{2n+2}\times P_{3})\simeq\mathrm{Ind}(\Gamma_{n,3})\ast\mathrm{Ind}(\Gamma_{n,3})\simeq\mathbb{S}^{2n+1}. Similarly, the line graph of the categorical product P 2 ​ n + 3 × P 3 P_{2n+3}\times P_{3} is the disjoint union of Λ n , 3 \Lambda_{n,3} and Γ ~ n , 3 \widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,3} . Therefore, using Proposition 2.1 , Claims 3.3 and 3.4 we get 𝖬 ⁡ ( P 2 ​ n + 3 × P 3 ) ≃ Ind ⁡ ( Λ n , 3 ) ∗ Ind ⁡ ( Γ ~ n , 3 ) ≃ ⋁ 2 n + 2 − 1 𝕊 2 ​ n + 1 . \mathsf{M}(P_{2n+3}\times P_{3})\simeq\mathrm{Ind}(\Lambda_{n,3})\ast\mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,3})\simeq\bigvee^{2^{n+2}-1}\mathbb{S}^{2n+1}. ∎ 4 Matching complexes of P n × P 4 P_{n}\times P_{4} In this section, we aim to determine the homotopy type of the matching complex of the categorical product P n × P 4 P_{n}\times P_{4} . The graph P n × P 4 P_{n}\times P_{4} is disconnected, and contains two connected components. Regardless of whether n n is odd or even, these respective components are isomorphic. For n ≥ 0 n\geq 0 , we denote the connected components of the line graphs of P 2 ​ n + 2 × P 4 P_{2n+2}\times P_{4} and P 2 ​ n + 3 × P 4 P_{2n+3}\times P_{4} by Γ n , 4 \Gamma_{n,4} (see Figure 4(a) ) and Γ ~ n , 4 \widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,4} (see Figure 4(b) ), respectively. Therefore, we have the following: 𝖬 ⁡ ( P 2 ​ n + 2 × P 4 ) ≃ Ind ⁡ ( Γ n , 4 ) ∗ Ind ⁡ ( Γ n , 4 ) , \mathsf{M}(P_{2n+2}\times P_{4})\simeq\mathrm{Ind}(\Gamma_{n,4})*\mathrm{Ind}(\Gamma_{n,4}), 𝖬 ⁡ ( P 2 ​ n + 3 × P 4 ) ≃ Ind ⁡ ( Γ ~ n , 4 ) ∗ Ind ⁡ ( Γ ~ n , 4 ) . \mathsf{M}(P_{2n+3}\times P_{4})\simeq\mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,4})*\mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,4}). During the computation of the homotopy type of the independence complexes of graphs Γ n , 4 \Gamma_{n,4} and Γ ~ n , 4 \widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,4} for n ≥ 0 n\geq 0 , we encounter several intermediary graphs denoted as A n , 4 , A ~ n , 4 , B n , 4 A_{n,4},\widetilde{A}_{n,4},B_{n,4} , and B ~ n , 4 \widetilde{B}_{n,4} . We show that the independence complexes of Γ n , 4 \Gamma_{n,4} and Γ ~ n , 4 \widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,4} are wedges of suspensions of these intermediary graphs. By induction on n n , we show that the independence complexes of Γ n , 4 \Gamma_{n,4} , Γ ~ n , 4 \widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,4} , and the intermediary graphs are homotopy equivalent to a wedge of spheres. The graphs A n , 4 , A ~ n , 4 , B n , 4 A_{n,4},\widetilde{A}_{n,4},B_{n,4} , and B ~ n , 4 \widetilde{B}_{n,4} are shown in Figures 5(a) , 5(b) , 6 and 7 , respectively. In the following subsection, we define the graphs Γ n , 4 \Gamma_{n,4} , Γ ~ n , 4 \widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,4} , along with four intermediary graphs. In the subsequent subsections, we then determine the homotopy types of the independence complexes of Γ n , 4 \Gamma_{n,4} , Γ ~ n , 4 \widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,4} , and these intermediary graphs. 4.1 B ~ n , 4 \widetilde{B}_{n,4} 4.2 Independence complexes of Γ n , 4 \Gamma_{n,4} and Γ ~ n , 4 \widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,4} In this section, we establish recursive relations among the independence complexes of the graphs in { Γ n , 4 , Γ ~ n , 4 , A n , 4 , A ~ n , 4 , B n , 4 , B ~ n , 4 } \{\Gamma_{n,4},\widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,4},A_{n,4},\widetilde{A}_{n,4},B_{n,4},\widetilde{B}_{n,4}\} . Claim 4.1 . For n ≥ 0 n\geq 0 , Ind ⁡ ( Γ n , 4 ) ≃ { 𝕊 0 if n = 0 , 𝕊 1 ∨ 𝕊 1 if n = 1 , Σ ​ Ind ​ ( A n − 1 , 4 ) ∨ Σ 2 ​ Ind ​ ( Γ ~ n − 2 , 4 ) if n ≥ 2 . \mathrm{Ind}(\Gamma_{n,4})\simeq\begin{cases}\mathbb{S}^{0}&\text{if $n=0$,}\\ \mathbb{S}^{1}\vee\mathbb{S}^{1}&\text{if $n=1$,}\\ \Sigma\mathrm{Ind}(A_{n-1,4})\vee\Sigma^{2}\mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{n-2,4})&\text{if $n\geq 2$.}\end{cases} Proof. Now, consider n ≥ 1 n\geq 1 . Using Claim 4.3 , we get Ind ⁡ ( Γ 1 , 4 ) ≃ 𝕊 1 ∨ 𝕊 1 \mathrm{Ind}(\Gamma_{1,4})\simeq\mathbb{S}^{1}\vee\mathbb{S}^{1} . Now, consider n ≥ 2 n\geq 2 . Thus, Ind ⁡ ( Γ n , 4 ) ≃ Σ ​ Ind ​ ( A n − 1 , 4 ) ∨ Σ 2 ​ Ind ​ ( Γ ~ n − 2 , 4 ) \mathrm{Ind}(\Gamma_{n,4})\simeq\Sigma\mathrm{Ind}(A_{n-1,4})\vee\Sigma^{2}\mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{n-2,4}) . ∎ Claim 4.2 . Therefore, Ind ⁡ ( Γ ~ 1 , 4 1 ) ≃ ⋁ 3 𝕊 2 \mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{1,4}^{1})\simeq\bigvee^{3}\mathbb{S}^{2} . This implies that Ind ( Γ ~ 1 , 4 ) ≃ ∨ 3 𝕊 2 \mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{1,4})\simeq\vee^{3}\mathbb{S}^{2} . Now, let n ≥ 2 n\geq 2 . Following similar reduction to the Claim 4.1 , we get Ind ⁡ ( Γ ~ n , 4 ) ≃ Σ ​ Ind ​ ( A ~ n − 1 , 4 ) ∨ Σ 2 ​ Ind ​ ( Γ n − 2 , 4 ) . \mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,4})\simeq\Sigma\mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{A}_{n-1,4})\vee\Sigma^{2}\mathrm{Ind}(\Gamma_{n-2,4}). ∎ Claim 4.3 . Now, the result follows from ( 4.1 ), ( 4.2 ) and ( 4.3 ). ∎ Claim 4.4 . For n ≥ 0 n\geq 0 , Ind ⁡ ( A ~ n , 4 ) ≃ { 𝕊 1 ∨ 𝕊 1 if n = 0 , ∨ 3 𝕊 2 if n = 1 , Σ ​ Ind ​ ( A ~ n − 1 , 4 ) ∨ Σ 2 ​ Ind ​ ( A n − 2 , 4 ) ∨ Σ 3 ​ Ind ​ ( B ~ n − 2 , 4 ) if n ≥ 2 . \mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{A}_{n,4})\simeq\begin{cases}\mathbb{S}^{1}\vee\mathbb{S}^{1}&\text{if $n=0$,}\\ \vee^{3}\mathbb{S}^{2}&\text{if $n=1$,}\\ \Sigma\mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{A}_{n-1,4})\vee\Sigma^{2}\mathrm{Ind}(A_{n-2,4})\vee\Sigma^{3}\mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{B}_{n-2,4})&\text{if $n\geq 2$.}\end{cases} Proof. The maximum and minimum dimension of spheres in the homotopy type of 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n × P 4 ) \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\times P_{4}) as given in Proposition 1.5 can be concluded from Proposition 4.9 . 5 Matching complexes of P n × P 5 P_{n}\times P_{5} In this section, we compute the homotopy type of the matching complex of the categorical product P n × P 5 P_{n}\times P_{5} . Since P n P_{n} is bipartite, P n × P 5 P_{n}\times P_{5} (and its line graph) is disconnected and contains exactly two connected components. When n n is even, these components are isomorphic; whereas, for odd n n , they are non-isomorphic. For n ≥ 0 n\geq 0 , we denote the connected component of the line graph of P 2 ​ n + 2 × P 5 P_{2n+2}\times P_{5} by Γ n , 5 \Gamma_{n,5} (see Figure 10 ). Since the line graph L ⁡ ( P 2 ​ n + 2 × P 5 ) L(P_{2n+2}\times P_{5}) is the same as Γ n , 5 ⊔ Γ n , 5 \Gamma_{n,5}\sqcup\Gamma_{n,5} , the matching complex 𝖬 ⁡ ( P 2 ​ n + 2 × P 5 ) = Ind ⁡ ( L ⁡ ( P 2 ​ n + 2 × P 5 ) ) ≃ Ind ⁡ ( Γ n , 5 ) ∗ Ind ⁡ ( Γ n , 5 ) . \mathsf{M}(P_{2n+2}\times P_{5})=\mathrm{Ind}(L(P_{2n+2}\times P_{5}))\simeq\mathrm{Ind}(\Gamma_{n,5})*\mathrm{Ind}(\Gamma_{n,5}). Similarly, for n ≥ 0 n\geq 0 , let Λ n , 5 \Lambda_{n,5} and Γ ~ n , 5 \widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,5} (see Figures 11 and 12 ) denote the two non-isomorphic connected components of the line graph of P 2 ​ n + 3 × P 5 P_{2n+3}\times P_{5} . Therefore, we have 𝖬 ⁡ ( P 2 ​ n + 3 × P 5 ) = Ind ⁡ ( L ⁡ ( P 2 ​ n + 3 × P 5 ) ) ≃ Ind ⁡ ( Λ n , 5 ) ∗ Ind ⁡ ( Γ ~ n , 5 ) . \mathsf{M}(P_{2n+3}\times P_{5})=\mathrm{Ind}(L(P_{2n+3}\times P_{5}))\simeq\mathrm{Ind}(\Lambda_{n,5})*\mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,5}). Finally, we give the minimum and maximum dimension of spheres in the homotopy type of 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n × P 5 ) \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\times P_{5}) in Section 5.3 . 5.1 Independence complexes of Γ n , 5 \Gamma_{n,5} and Λ n , 5 \Lambda_{n,5} In this section, we introduce 6 6 new classes of graphs, namely A n , 5 , B n , 5 , C n , 5 , D n , 5 , A_{n,5},B_{n,5},C_{n,5},D_{n,5}, E n , 5 E_{n,5} , and F n , 5 F_{n,5} for n ≥ 0 n\geq 0 . We show that the independence complexes of Γ n , 5 \Gamma_{n,5} and Λ n , 5 \Lambda_{n,5} can be expressed in terms of wedges of suspensions of independence complexes of these intermediary graphs. Using induction on n n , we establish that the independence complexes of Γ n , 5 \Gamma_{n,5} , Λ n , 5 \Lambda_{n,5} , and these intermediary graphs are homotopy equivalent to a wedge of spheres. Proof. Since Γ 0 , 5 \Gamma_{0,5} is a path with 4-vertices, by Proposition 2.7 , we have Ind ⁡ ( Γ 0 , 5 ) \mathrm{Ind}(\Gamma_{0,5}) is homotopy equivalent to a point. For any graph G n , 5 ∈ { Γ n , 5 , Λ n , 5 , A n , 5 , B n , 5 , C n , 5 , D n , 5 , E n , 5 , F n , 5 : n ≥ 0 } G_{n,5}\in\{\Gamma_{n,5},\Lambda_{n,5},A_{n,5},B_{n,5},C_{n,5},D_{n,5},E_{n,5},F_{n,5}:n\geq 0\} , Ind ⁡ ( G n , 5 ) \mathrm{Ind}(G_{n,5}) is homotopy equivalent to a wedge of spheres. Proof. For any graph G 0 , 5 ∈ { Γ 0 , 5 , Λ 0 , 5 , A 0 , 5 , B 0 , 5 , C 0 , 5 , D 0 , 5 , E 0 , 5 , F 0 , 5 } G_{0,5}\in\{\Gamma_{0,5},\Lambda_{0,5},A_{0,5},B_{0,5},C_{0,5},D_{0,5},E_{0,5},F_{0,5}\} , Ind ⁡ ( G 0 , 5 ) \mathrm{Ind}(G_{0,5}) is homotopy equivalent to a wedge of spheres from Claims 5.4 to 5.11 . Now using induction on n n , and recursive relations established in Claims 5.4 to 5.11 , we conclude that, for any graph G n , 5 ∈ { Γ n , 5 , Λ n , 5 , A n , 5 , B n , 5 , C n , 5 , D n , 5 , E n , 5 , F n , 5 : n ≥ 0 } G_{n,5}\in\{\Gamma_{n,5},\Lambda_{n,5},A_{n,5},B_{n,5},C_{n,5},D_{n,5},E_{n,5},F_{n,5}:n\geq 0\} , Ind ⁡ ( G n , 5 ) \mathrm{Ind}(G_{n,5}) is homotopy equivalent to a wedge of spheres. ∎ 5.2 Independence complex of Γ ~ n , 5 \widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,5} Recall that for n ≥ 0 n\geq 0 , the line graph of P 2 ​ n + 3 × P 5 P_{2n+3}\times P_{5} comprises of two connected components Λ n , 5 \Lambda_{n,5} and Γ ~ n , 5 \widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,5} (see Figures 11 and 12 ). The computation of Ind ⁡ ( Γ ~ n , 5 ) \mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,5}) follows a similar approach as that of Ind ⁡ ( Γ n , 5 ) \mathrm{Ind}(\Gamma_{n,5}) . The intermediary graphs occurring during the computation of Ind ⁡ ( Γ ~ n , 5 ) \mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,5}) resemble those defined in Section 5.1.1 , with an inclusion of certain additional vertices and edges. For each H n , 5 ∈ { Γ n , 5 , Λ n , 5 , A n , 5 , B n , 5 , C n , 5 , D n , 5 , E n , 5 , F n , 5 } H_{n,5}\in\{\Gamma_{n,5},\Lambda_{n,5},A_{n,5},B_{n,5},C_{n,5},D_{n,5},E_{n,5},F_{n,5}\} , where n ∈ ℕ ∪ { 0 } n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\} , we define V ⁡ ( H ~ n , 5 ) = V ⁡ ( H n , 5 ) ∪ { g 1 2 ​ n + 2 , g 2 2 ​ n + 2 , g 3 2 ​ n + 2 , g 4 2 ​ n + 2 } , E ⁡ ( H ~ n , 5 ) = E ( H n , 5 ) ∪ { g i ​ 2 ​ n + 1 g i ​ 2 ​ n + 2 : i = 1 , 2 , 3 , 4 } ∪ { g i ​ 2 ​ n + 2 g i + 1 2 ​ n + 2 : i = 1 , 2 , 3 } ∪ { g 2 2 ​ n + 1 ​ g 3 2 ​ n + 2 , g 3 2 ​ n + 1 ​ g 2 2 ​ n + 2 } . \begin{split}V(\widetilde{H}_{n,5})=&V(H_{n,5})\cup\{g_{1\,2n+2},g_{2\,2n+2},g_{3\,2n+2},g_{4\,2n+2}\},\\ E(\widetilde{H}_{n,5})=&E(H_{n,5})\cup\{g_{i\,2n+1}g_{i\,2n+2}:i=1,2,3,4\}\cup\{g_{i\,2n+2}g_{i+1\,2n+2}:i=1,2,3\}\\ &\cup\{g_{2\,2n+1}g_{3\,2n+2},g_{3\,2n+1}g_{2\,2n+2}\}.\end{split} In this section, we compute the independence complexes of graphs Γ ~ n , 5 , Λ ~ n , 5 , A ~ n , 5 , B ~ n , 5 , \widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,5},\widetilde{\Lambda}_{n,5},\widetilde{A}_{n,5},\widetilde{B}_{n,5}, C ~ n , 5 \widetilde{C}_{n,5} , D ~ n , 5 , E ~ n , 5 \widetilde{D}_{n,5},\widetilde{E}_{n,5} , and F ~ n , 5 \widetilde{F}_{n,5} . In computing the independence complexes of these graphs, as discussed in Section 5.1.2 , our approach focuses on performing computations primarily on one end (the leftmost part) of the graph while keeping the other end (the rightmost part) fixed. Notably, for n ≥ 1 n\geq 1 , observe that the left ends of the graphs H n , 5 H_{n,5} and H ~ n , 5 \widetilde{H}_{n,5} coincide, where H n , 5 ∈ { Γ n , 5 , Λ n , 5 , A n , 5 , B n , 5 , C n , 5 , D n , 5 , E n , 5 , F n , 5 } H_{n,5}\in\{\Gamma_{n,5},\Lambda_{n,5},A_{n,5},B_{n,5},C_{n,5},D_{n,5},E_{n,5},F_{n,5}\} . This observation suggests a method for computing the independence complexes of graphs Γ ~ n , 5 , Λ ~ n , 5 , A ~ n , 5 , B ~ n , 5 , C ~ n , 5 , D ~ n , 5 , E ~ n , 5 \widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,5},\widetilde{\Lambda}_{n,5},\widetilde{A}_{n,5},\widetilde{B}_{n,5},\widetilde{C}_{n,5},\widetilde{D}_{n,5},\widetilde{E}_{n,5} , and F ~ n , 5 \widetilde{F}_{n,5} using a similar strategy to that outlined in Section 5.1.2 . Furthermore, for n ≥ 1 n\geq 1 , these intermediary graphs adhere to a similar recurrence relation as those described in the preceding section. Since the base graphs of these intermediary graphs are completely different from the graphs in Section 5.1.1 , we provide the complete computations for base cases. Thus, combining all of these, we have the following: (5.26) Ind ⁡ ( E ~ n , 5 ) ≃ { 𝕊 1 for n = 0 , ⋁ 5 𝕊 3 for n = 1 , ( ⋁ 2 Σ 3 ​ Ind ​ ( A ~ n − 2 , 5 ) ) ∨ ( ⋁ 2 Σ 3 ​ Ind ​ ( F ~ n − 2 , 5 ) ) ∨ Σ ​ Ind ​ ( A ~ n − 1 , 5 ) ∨ Ind ⁡ ( Γ ~ n , 5 ) for n ≥ 2 . \mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{E}_{n,5})\simeq\begin{cases}\mathbb{S}^{1}&\text{for $n=0$,}\\ \bigvee^{5}\mathbb{S}^{3}&\text{for $n=1$,}\\ \left({\bigvee}^{2}\Sigma^{3}\mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{A}_{n-2,5})\right)\vee\left({\bigvee}^{2}\Sigma^{3}\mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{F}_{n-2,5})\right)\vee\Sigma\mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{A}_{n-1,5})\vee\mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,5})&\text{for $n\geq 2$.}\end{cases} • Ind ⁡ ( F ~ n , 5 ) \mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{F}_{n,5}) : Since N ⁡ ( g 12 ) ⊆ N ⁡ ( g 21 ) N(g_{12})\subseteq N(g_{21}) , and N ⁡ ( g 42 ) ⊆ N ⁡ ( g 31 ) N(g_{42})\subseteq N(g_{31}) in graphs F ~ 0 , 5 \widetilde{F}_{0,5} , and F ~ 0 , 5 ∖ g 21 \widetilde{F}_{0,5}\setminus g_{21} , respectively, from Proposition 2.3 , Ind ⁡ ( F ~ 0 , 5 ) ≃ Ind ⁡ ( F ~ 0 , 5 ∖ { g 21 , g 31 } ) \mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{F}_{0,5})\simeq\mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{F}_{0,5}\setminus\{g_{21},g_{31}\}) . Both the graphs ( F ~ 0 , 5 ∖ { g 21 , g 31 } ) ∖ N ⁡ [ g 41 ] (\widetilde{F}_{0,5}\setminus\{g_{21},g_{31}\})\setminus N[g_{41}] and ( F ~ 0 , 5 ∖ { g 21 , g 31 } ) ∖ N ⁡ [ f 2 ] (\widetilde{F}_{0,5}\setminus\{g_{21},g_{31}\})\setminus N[f_{2}] are isomorphic to P 5 P_{5} . Hence, Ind ⁡ ( F ~ 0 , 5 ) ≃ 𝕊 2 ∨ 𝕊 2 \mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{F}_{0,5})\simeq\mathbb{S}^{2}\vee\mathbb{S}^{2} . For n ≥ 1 n\geq 1 , the relation for Ind ⁡ ( F ~ n , 5 ) \mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{F}_{n,5}) follows from a similar argument as that of Claim 5.11 . For any graph G ~ n , 5 ∈ { Γ ~ n , 5 , Λ ~ n , 5 , A ~ n , 5 , B ~ n , 5 , C ~ n , 5 , D ~ n , 5 , E ~ n , 5 , F ~ n , 5 : n ≥ 0 } \widetilde{G}_{n,5}\in\{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{n,5},\widetilde{\Lambda}_{n,5},\widetilde{A}_{n,5},\widetilde{B}_{n,5},\widetilde{C}_{n,5},\widetilde{D}_{n,5},\widetilde{E}_{n,5},\widetilde{F}_{n,5}:n\geq 0\} , Ind ⁡ ( G ~ n , 5 ) \mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{G}_{n,5}) is homotopy equivalent to a wedge of spheres. Proof. The result follows from ( 5.20 ) to ( 5.27 ), Theorem 5.12 and induction on n n . ∎ 5.3 Homotopy type of 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n × P 5 ) \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\times P_{5}) In this section, we conclude the homotopy type of 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n × P 5 ) \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\times P_{5}) . We also determine the maximum and minimum dimensions of the spheres occurring in the homotopy type of the independence complexes of the graphs in { Γ n , 5 , Λ n , 5 , A n , 5 , B n , 5 \{\Gamma_{n,5},\Lambda_{n,5},A_{n,5},B_{n,5} , C n , 5 , D n , 5 , E n , 5 , F n , 5 } C_{n,5},D_{n,5},E_{n,5},F_{n,5}\} . Theorem 5.14 . For n ≥ 2 n\geq 2 , 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n × P 5 ) \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\times P_{5}) is homotopy equivalent to a wedge of spheres. Proof. Case 1: If n n is even, i.e., n = 2 ​ k n=2k for some positive integer k k , then P n × P 5 P_{n}\times P_{5} comprises of two isomorphic connected components whose line graphs are isomorphic to Γ k − 1 , 5 \Gamma_{k-1,5} . Thus, 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n × P 5 ) ≃ Ind ⁡ ( Γ k − 1 , 5 ) ∗ Ind ⁡ ( Γ k − 1 , 5 ) . \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\times P_{5})\simeq\mathrm{Ind}(\Gamma_{k-1,5})\ast\mathrm{Ind}(\Gamma_{k-1,5}). Now, the result follows from Theorem 5.12 . Case 2: If n n is odd, i.e., n = 2 ​ k + 1 n=2k+1 for some positive integer k k , then P n × P 5 P_{n}\times P_{5} comprises of two non-isomorphic connected components with corresponding line graphs isomorphic to Λ k − 1 , 5 \Lambda_{k-1,5} and Γ ~ k − 1 , 5 \widetilde{\Gamma}_{k-1,5} . Thus, 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n × P 5 ) ≃ Ind ⁡ ( Λ k − 1 , 5 ) ∗ Ind ⁡ ( Γ ~ k − 1 , 5 ) . \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\times P_{5})\simeq\mathrm{Ind}(\Lambda_{k-1,5})\ast\mathrm{Ind}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{k-1,5}). The result follows from Theorems 5.12 and 5.13 . ∎ Proposition 5.15 . The maximum and minimum dimension of spheres in the homotopy type of 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n × P 5 ) \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\times P_{5}) as given in Proposition 1.4 can be concluded from Tables 6 to 9 . 6 Future Directions We proved that for n ≥ 3 n\geq 3 and 3 ≤ m ≤ 5 3\leq m\leq 5 , the matching complex of P n × P m P_{n}\times P_{m} is homotopy equivalent to a wedge of spheres. For m = 3 m=3 , we explicitly compute the number and dimension of spheres appearing in the wedge. Further, for m ∈ { 4 , 5 } m\in\{4,5\} , we have given the minimum and maximum dimension of spheres appearing in the wedge in 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n × P m ) \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\times P_{m}) . This exploration naturally leads to the following question: Question 6.1 . Can we determine the dimensions of the spheres appearing in the wedge representing the homotopy types of the matching complexes of categorical products P n × P 4 P_{n}\times P_{4} and P n × P 5 P_{n}\times P_{5} ? Based on the main result of this article and our computer-based computations, we propose the following: Conjecture 6.2 . For n ≥ 3 n\geq 3 and m ≥ 6 m\geq 6 , the matching complex of categorical product P n × P m P_{n}\times P_{m} is homotopy equivalent to a wedge of spheres. For odd m m and n n , one of the components of P n × P m P_{n}\times P_{m} is an induced subgraph of P n ​ □ ​ P m P_{n}\Box P_{m} (see Figure 1 ). Since the homotopy type of the matching complexes of P n ​ □ ​ P m P_{n}\Box P_{m} is not known for m ≥ 3 m\geq 3 , it will be interesting to see if there is any relation between the topology of 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n × P n ) \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\times P_{n}) and 𝖬 ⁡ ( P n ​ □ ​ P m ) \mathsf{M}(P_{n}\Box P_{m}) . Question 6.3 . Is there any correlation between the matching complexes of the cartesian product of path graphs and those of the categorical product of path graphs? 7 Acknowledgment The authors would like to thank the anonymous referees for many useful comments and suggestions. Instructions for reporting errors We are continuing to improve HTML versions of papers, and your feedback helps enhance accessibility and mobile support. To report errors in the HTML that will help us improve conversion and rendering, choose any of the methods listed below: Click the "Report Issue" ( ) button, located in the page header. Tip: You can select the relevant text first, to include it in your report. Our team has already identified the following issues . We appreciate your time reviewing and reporting rendering errors we may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us improve the HTML versions for all readers, because disability should not be a barrier to accessing research. Thank you for your continued support in championing open access for all. Have a free development cycle? Help support accessibility at arXiv!
